<div class="post" data-post-id="35779" data-author="Teron" data-date="2015-03-30T18:48:22+00:00">

<23hp 353ma 146mv> 3434381940, -4, 5.8, indoors You reluctantly give some items to the First Weaver, who swallows them with appetite.

The First Weaver chews on the items for a while, then his web-sacks produce

a wondrous masterpiece fashioned of scintillating silk.

You carefully snatch scintillating silken armor from the First Weaver.

<23hp 353ma 146mv> 3434381940, -4, 5.8, indoors The First Weaver finally begins to pay attention to the surroundings.  Uh oh...

The First Weaver utters the words, 'xzb'

You are covered in sticky webs!

You have been KILLED!! 

Gossameringue is fun.

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="35785" data-author="*Neptune*" data-date="2015-04-01T14:04:41+00:00">

That seems to have happened quite a bit, but make no mistake: it's by design.

On the other hand, I've slightly reduced the chance that will happen to help to

make that situation a bit more manageable. I hope this helps.

-Neptune

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="35788" data-author="Teron" data-date="2015-04-01T18:55:22+00:00">

Yes, I understand it's by design, though it did happen within 1-3 rounds after starting the fight.

Thanks for the fix :)

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="35790" data-author="Yasik" data-date="2015-04-03T05:51:25+00:00">

Do I see text lines appear in the prompt line, not at the beginning?

How do you do your triggers then, not using the ^ or $ in regexps, marking start and end of lines, correspondingly?

Can fix that by typing "compact prompt", I think.

Btw, modern Internet connections wide enough so this option can be set at character creation, personally I dont see why this can be turned off.

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="35791" data-author="Teron" data-date="2015-04-04T17:24:39+00:00">

Zmud has an option to trigger, when a buffer of text is received. It doesn't have to be a new line, though in this case, practically, it always is.

Yes, the new info is shown on a side of the prompt, since because it's a continuation of the prompt and I need it this way.

I already have compact prompt/lines enabled.

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="37178" data-author="*juggleblood*" data-date="2016-08-05T23:17:31+00:00">

Dunno if its the simplest/most elegant solution, but I made a 5 second cooldown timer and made the secondWind function checkit before executing, so then in scripts I can just use secondWind() wherever and not worry about that.

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="37183" data-author="Teron" data-date="2016-08-06T10:14:32+00:00">

For backstabs I add variable checks, if the event to fire has already occured since the stab, so the web gets thrown on the first seen backstab/whiff, and so does second wind now. It works fine and I don't need timers (not that my old Zmud is good with them, anyway).

Also, 5 seconds seems like a lot, isn't the round 3 seconds?

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="37192" data-author="*juggleblood*" data-date="2016-08-07T14:13:06+00:00">

The 5 seconds is to account for lag and the finite amount of movement points. 

This is on Kung and to give an example, I have a script that throws out doublestrikes or doublebroadsides if I have the moves for it. So yeah with 5 second cooldown he doesn't get one out every round, but then he wouldn't have the moves to do that anyway.

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="37228" data-author="cyprimus" data-date="2016-08-12T08:39:55+00:00">

Defense Attorney: Will you please state your age?

Little Old Lady: I am 86 years old.

Defense Attorney: Will you tell us, in your own words, what happened the night of April 1st?

Little Old Lady: There I was, sitting there in my swing on my front porch on a warm spring evening, when a young man comes creeping up on the porch and sat down beside me.

Defense Attorney: Did you know him?

Little Old Lady: No, but he sure was friendly.

Defense Attorney: What happened after he sat down?

Little Old Lady: He started to rub my thigh.

Defense Attorney: Did you stop him?

Little Old Lady: No, I didnâ€™t stop him.

Defense Attorney: Why not?

Little Old Lady: It felt good. Nobody had done that since my Albert died some 30 years ago.

Defense Attorney: What happened next?

Little Old Lady: He began to rub my breasts.

Defense Attorney: Did you stop him then?

Little Old Lady: No, I did not stop him.

Defense Attorney: Why not?

Little Old Lady: His rubbing made me feel all alive and excited. I havenâ€™t felt that good in years!

Defense Attorney: What happened next?

Little Old Lady: Well, by then, I was feeling so â€œspicyâ€ that I just laid down and told him â€œTake me, young man. Take me now!â€

Defense Attorney: Did he take you?

Little Old Lady: Hell, no! He just yelled, â€œApril Fools!â€ And thatâ€™s when I shot him, the little bastard.

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="37244" data-author="cyprimus" data-date="2016-08-13T21:07:14+00:00">

A priest and a nun were lost in a blizzard. After a while, they came upon a small cabin. Being exhausted, they prepared to go to sleep. There was a stack of blankets and a sleeping bag on the floor â€“ but only one bed.

Being a gentleman, the priest said, â€œSister, you sleep on the bed. Iâ€™ll sleep on the floor in the sleeping bag.â€

Just as he got zipped up in the bag and was beginning to fall asleep, the nun said â€œFather, Iâ€™m cold.â€

He unzipped the sleeping bag, got up, got a blanket and put it on her.

Once again, he got into the sleeping bag, zipped it up and started to drift off to sleep when the nun once again said, â€œFather, Iâ€™m still very cold.â€

He unzipped the bag, got up again, put another blanket on her and got into the sleeping bag once again. Just as his eyes closed, she said, â€œFather, Iâ€™m sooooo cold.â€

This time, he remained there, giving the woman a wink and a smile, then said, â€œSister, I have an idea. Weâ€™re out here in the wilderness where no one will ever know what happened. Letâ€™s pretend weâ€™re married.â€

The nun purred, â€œSounds good to me.â€

To which the priest yelled out, â€œOkay then â€“ get up and get your own stupid blanket!â€

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="37249" data-author="cyprimus" data-date="2016-08-15T16:09:20+00:00">

A man walks into a restaurant with a full-grown ostrich behind him. The waitress asks for their orders.

The man says, â€œA hamburger, fries, and a coke,â€ and turns to the ostrich, â€œWhatâ€™s yours?â€ â€œIâ€™ll have the same,â€ says the ostrich. A short time later the waitress returns with the order. â€œThat will be $9.40 please,â€ and the man reaches into his pocket and pulls out the exact change for payment.

The next day, the man and the ostrich come again and the man says, â€œA hamburger, fries, and a coke.â€ The ostrich says, â€œIâ€™ll have the same.â€ Again the man reaches into his pocket and pays with exact change.

This becomes routine until, the two enter again. â€œThe usual?â€ asks the waitress.â€No, this is Friday night, so I will have a steak, baked potato, and salad, says he man â€œSame,â€ says the ostrich.

Shortly the waitress brings the order and says, â€œThat will be $32.62.â€ Once again the man pulls the exact change out of his pocket and places it on the table. The waitress canâ€™t hold back her curiosity any longer. â€œExcuse me, sir. How do you manage to always come up with the exact change out of your pocket every time?â€

â€œWell,â€ says the man, â€œseveral years ago I was cleaning the attic and found an old lamp. When I rubbed it a Genie appeared and offered me two wishes. My first wish was that if I ever had to pay for anything, I would just put my hand in my pocket and the right amount of money would always be there.â€

â€œThatâ€™s brilliant!â€ says the waitressâ€¦ â€œMost people would wish for a million dollars or something, but youâ€™ll always be as rich as you want for as long as you live!â€

â€œThatâ€™s right. Whether itâ€™s a gallon of milk or a Rolls Royce, the exact money is always there,â€ says the man. The waitress asks, â€œBut, sir, whatâ€™s with the ostrich?â€

The man sighs, pauses, and answers, â€œMy second wish was for a tall chick with long legs who agrees with everything I say.â€

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="37978" data-author="Teron" data-date="2017-01-18T00:13:42+00:00">

You guys seem to write as if there's a problem. There isn't. Just funny being splorked, that's all.

**Josiah**, recently I've been thinking if the dr cap difference between wa/mo and wa/dr/mo is worth it.  Maybe in these cases it is.

**Gorka**, it's ok, I hope you learned to move fast there.

**JB**, yes, that's two baboons.

My dilemma was to get a bard to sing luck for others (the zeridan leader doesn't repop with ppl at him) or to stay in Taron and chop faster. If I went in as a bard, chances are I'd have been splorked there, if I'd had failed a single cast of reign of confusion. In fact, I ran there once as a bard, a few mobs tried to kill me and nearly succeeded - and then I learned the leader doesn't repop, when you are standing there.

If you mean that I should call you next time I go to Mystica, sure, just keep the smartphone w/ Telegram nearby .)

Oh, one more thing. I was splorked, when I was at a gateable spot and was gating the group. Of course, I could've moved, but then someone else would have been splorked. I'm not convinced that having anyone but you, Oboe or Blackmore in the group would've helped. As Josiah said, it's a tough area.

</div>

<div class="post" data-post-id="39626" data-author="Gorka" data-date="2020-11-25T09:50:20+00:00">

Sorry to burst your bubble Baby (RE: Monk Power), but I think you'll find Koa did nearly all of the damage with a massive critical chop on the excruciating blow. You were lucky there was anything left to kill...

As funny as this is, it also kind of makes a mockery of the game, when you consider the time it takes to get the xp gained from that 8 second kill is exponentially longer in the "normal game." To everything there is an opposite and equal reaction, and the wanton excess of killing xerxes translates to less game credibility and lazier players.

</div>
